# Stale source notes: sourceReferences, referencesAtStart and referencesAtEnd

Checked 2026-11-03. Scope: `curriculum/courses/**` and `curriculum/shared/**` JSON, fields `sourceReferences`, `referencesAtStart` and `referencesAtEnd` only. Learner-visible text (for example `point` fields) is not in scope. Report only: no lesson or licence file was edited, nothing was committed.

## Counts

- Rows in table: 1139 across 526 files.
- STALE: 6
- STILL VALID: 178
- NEEDS SOURCE: 955

## Method

- Walked every JSON file under `courses/` and `shared/` and kept string values under the three named fields that carry a stale or open marker: re-verified, re-checked, not re-checked/re-read/re-verified, not confirmed, not yet confirmed, not verified, not checked, PRIOR, PARTIAL, UNCONFIRMED, not yet checked, plus the plain `checked 2026-...` date where it sits with one of those markers.
- The plain `checked 2026-...` pattern alone matches about 3,000 lines, almost all of them positive records or matrix status tags. Those were not treated as stale notes. Positive dated records are listed only where they sit beside an open marker.
- Each row was then checked against the research notes and the flagged lessons. STALE means today's checks already confirmed or replaced the claim the note says is open. STILL VALID means the note is an accurate disclaimer, removal record, cross-reference or draft provenance line. NEEDS SOURCE means the note names a point that is still open and no check recorded in today's research files closes it.
- Current text is cut at 170 characters. Locate the full text with the file and field.

## Decisions to review (decided without asking)

1. The five boom-type notes flagged by the boom agent are STALE. Their sentence says NSW, QLD, WA, TAS and NT thresholds were "not re-verified" and that numeric thresholds were removed. Both are now wrong: the matrix rows were read on 2026-11-02 and the lesson text states the thresholds with R90 and R91 entries checked 2026-11-03. The ACT part stays open because the Access Canberra page was not read.
2. `roof-cleaning-soft-washing-L03` has a removal note saying boom thresholds were "not re-checked in this session". Its reason is stale for NSW, QLD, WA, TAS and NT, so it is marked STALE with a replacement that keeps ACT open. The lesson text itself states no threshold and needs no change.
3. `lessons/ready/...` provenance lines that say "source draft, not verified; checked 2026-11-02" are STILL VALID. No later check of the draft text was found, and the research matrix checks do not verify draft wording.
4. Disclaimers in `referencesAtStart` and `referencesAtEnd` that say a point is "not yet confirmed" or "check before use" are STILL VALID. They make no claim the lesson relies on.
5. Removal records ("removed from this lesson", "removed from officialReferences") are STILL VALID.

## Items that need the owner

- `curriculum/research/trades/LICENCE-MATRIX-TAS-ACT-NT.csv` row ACT-22 is still CONFIRMED (checked 2026-11-02), but `curriculum/research/BOOM-OTHER-STATES.md` says the Access Canberra page was not read. This row needs a source or a status change. Lesson text in the five boom files already omits ACT.
- `pressure-cleaning-L01` `sourceReferences[16]` says the Energy Safe Victoria PV installer guideline was "Not read" because robots rules blocked it. The same URL is cited as CONFIRMED in `LICENCE-MATRIX.csv` VIC-24 and VIC-26 (checked 2026-11-02) and in about 100 lessons. Both cannot stand. Decide which record is right.
- `shared/safety/WAH-07` `sourceReferences[13]` and the four exterior boom lessons: the replacement text is in the table. The lesson body text was not checked here (out of scope).
- Several NEEDS SOURCE rows name the same missing sources: the NSW EPA stormwater page, the Safe Work Australia SDS page (fetch refused), the 2014 overhead-lines guide, FSANZ Standard 3.2.1, the Safe Work Australia falls model code (PDF blocked), the Schedule 2 ACL text, and the NHMRC clause text. Each needs one primary read, then the replacement in the table applies to all rows that cite it.
